title: First AOU CHECKLIST

page: 73

order: Pygopodes: Diving Birds
suborder: Podicipedes: Grebes
family: Podicipidae: Grebes

genus: Aechmophorus: Coues
Aechmophorus: Coues, Pr. Ac. Nat. Sci. Phila. April, 1862, 229.
type: Podiceps occidentalis: Lawr.

species: Aechmophorus occidentalis: (Lawr.): Western Grebe
Podiceps occidentalis Lawr. in Baird's B. N. Am. 1858, 894.
Aechmophorus occidentalis Coues, Pr. Ac. Nat. Sci. Phila. 1862, 229.
habitat: Western North America, eastward to Manitoba.

genus: Colymbus: Linnaeus
Colymbus: Linn. S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 135.
type: by elimination, Colymbus cristatus Linn.

subgenus: Colymbus

species: Colymbus holboellii: (Reinh.): Holboell's Grebe.
Podiceps holboellii Reinh. Vid. Med. 1853, 76.
Colymbus holboellii Ridgway, Water B. N. Am. II. 1884, 428.
habitat: North America at large, including Greenland. Also Eastern Siberia, and southward to Japan. Breeds in high latitudes, migrating south in winter.

page: 74
subgenus: Dytes: Kaup.
Dytes: Kaup, Sk. Ent. Eur. Thierw. 1829, 49.
type: Colymbus auritus Linn.

species: Colymbus auritus: Linnaeus: Horned Grebe
Colymbus auritus Linnaeus. S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 135.
habitat: Northern Hemisphere. Breeds from the Northern United States northward.

subspecies: Colymbus nigricollis californicus: (Heerm.): American Eared Grebe
Podiceps californicus Heerm. Pr. Ac. Nat. Sci. Phila. 1854, 179.
Colymbus nigricollis californicus Ridgw. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. VIII. 1885, 356.
habitat: Northern and Western North America, from the Mississippi Valley westward.

subgenus: Podiceps Latham.
Podiceps Latham. Ind. Orn. II. 1790, 780.
type: by elimination, Colymbus fluviatilis Tunst.

species: Colymbus dominicus: Linnaeus: St. Domingo Grebe
Colymbus dominicus Linnaeus. S. N. ed. 12, I. 1766, 223.
habitat: Texas and Southern California southward through Tropical America to Paraguay, including the West Indies.

page: 75
genus: Podilymbus: Lesson
Podilymbus Lesson. Traite, I. 1831, 595.
type: Colymbus podiceps Linnaeus.

species: Podilymbus podiceps: (Linnaeus): Pied-billed Grebe
Colymbiis podiceps Linnaeus. S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 136.
Podilymbus podiceps Lawr. in Baird's B. N. Am. 1858, 898.
habitat: British Provinces southward to Brazil, Buenos Ayres, and Chili, including the West Indies and the Bermudas, breeding nearly throughout its range.

suborder: Cepphi: Loons and Auks

family: Urinatoridae: Loons

genus: Urinator: Cuvier.
Urinator Cuv. Anat. Comp. I. 1799, tabl. ii.
type: Colymbus imber Gunn.

species: Urinator imber: (Gunnerus): Loon
Colymbus imber Gunnerus, Trondh. Selsk. Skr. I. 1761, pi. iii.
Urinator imber Stejn. Orn. Expl. Kamtschat. 1885, 313.
habitat: Northern part of Northern Hemisphere. In North America breeds from the northern tier of States northward; ranges in winter south to the Gulf of Mexico.

species: Urinator adamsii: (Gray): Yellow-billed Loon.
Colymbus adamsii Gray, P. Z. S. 1859, 167.
Urinator adamsii Stejn. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. V. 1882, 43.
habitat: Arctic America, west of Hudson's Bay. Casual in Northern Europe and Asia.

page: 76
species: Urinator arcticus: (Linnaeus): Black-throated Loon.
Colymbus arcticus Linnaeus. S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 135.
Urinator arcticus Stejn. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. V. 1882, 43.
habitat: Northern part of the Northern Hemisphere. In North America migrating south in winter to the Northern United States.

species: Urinator pacificus: (Lawr.): Pacific Loon.
Colymbus pacificus Lawr. in Baird's B. N. Am. 1858, 889.
Urinator pacificus Stejn. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. V. 1882, 43.
habitat: Pacific coast of North America, south in winter to Cape St. Lucas and Guadalupe Island.

species: Urinator lumme: (Gunn.): Red-throated Loon.
Colymbus lumme Gunn. Trond. Selsk. Skr. I. 1761, pl. ii. fig. 2.
Urinator lumme Stejn. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. V. 1882, 43.
habitat: Northern part of Northern Hemisphere, migrating southward in winter nearly across the United States.

family: Alcidae: Auks, Murres, and Puffins

subfamily: Fraterculinae: Puffins

genus: Lunda: Pallas
Lunda Pall. Zoog. Rosso- As. II. 1826, 363.
type: Alca cirrhata: Pall.

page: 77
species: Lunda cirrhata: Pallas: Tufted Puffin.
Alca cirrhata Pallas. Spic. Zool. V. 1769, 7, pi. i., pi. ii. figs. I, 2, 3.
Lunda cirrhata Pallas. Zoog. Rosso-As. II. 1826, 363, pi. 82.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific from California to Alaska, and from Japan to Bering's Strait. Accidental on the coast of Maine.

genus: Fratercula: Brisson
Fratercula Brisson. Orn. VI. 1760, Si.
type: Alca arctica Linnaeus.

species: Fratercula arctica: (Linnaeus): Puffin
Alca arctica Linn. S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 13.
Fratercula arctica Schaffer, Mus. Orn. 1789, 61.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Atlantic, breeding on the North American coast from the Bay of Fundy northward. South in winter to Long Island, and casually further.

subspecies: Fratercula arctica glacialis: (Temminck.): Large-billed Puffin
Mormon glacialis "Leach," Temminck. Man. d'Orn. 2d ed. II. 1820, 933.
Fratercula arctica greek:beta glacialis Blasius, List B. Europ. 1862, 24.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the Arctic Ocean, from Spitzbergen to Baffin's Bay.

species: Fratercula corniculata: (Naumann): Horned Puffin.
Mormon corniculata Naumann, Isis, 1821, 782, pi. vii figs. 3, 4.
Fratercula corniculata Brandt, Bull. Ac. St. Pdtersb. II. 1837, 348.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific, from the Kurile Islands to Sitka.

page: 78
subfamily: Phalerinae: Auklets, Murrelets, Guillemots

genus: Cerorhinca: Bonaparte
Cerorhinca Bonaparte. Ann. Lyc. N. Y. 1828, 427. Type, C. occidentalis
Bonaparte = Alca monocerata Pallas

species: Cerorhinca monocerata: (Pallas): Rhinoceros Auklet
Alca monocerata Pallas. Zoog. Rosso-As. II. 1826, 362.
Cerorhina monocerata Cass. in Baird's B. N. Am. 1858, 905.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific, breeding southward to California and Japan ; in winter, southward to Lower California.

genus: Ptychoramphus: Brandt
Ptychoramphus Brandt, Bull. Ac. St. Petersb. II. 1837, 347.
type: Uria aleutica Pallas.

species: Ptychoramphus aleuticus (Pallas): Cassin's Auklet
Uria aleutica Pallas. Zoog. Rosso-As. II. 1726, 370.
Ptychoramphus aleuticus Brandt, Bull. Ac. St. Petersb. II. 1837, 347.
habitat: Pacific coast of North America, from the Aleutian Islands to San Diego, breeding southward to the Farallones.

genus: Cyclorrhynchus Kaup.
Cyclorrhynchus Kaup, Sk. Ent. Eur. Thierw. 1829, 15.
type: Alca psittacula Pallas.

species: Cyclorrhynchus psittaculus: (Pallas): Paroquet Auklet.
Alca psittacula Pall. Spic. Zool. V. 1760, 13, pi. ii., pi. v. figs. 4-6.
Cyclorliynchus psittaculus Stejn. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. VII. Aug. 5, 1884, 216.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific, from the Aleutian and Kurile Islands northward.

page: 79
genus: Simorhynchus: Merrem
subgenus: Simorhynchus
Simorhynchus Merrem, in Ersch & Gruber's Encycl. I sect. II. 1819, 405.
type: Alca cristatella Pallas.

Simorhynchus: cristatellus: (Pallas): Crested Auklet
Alca cristatella Pallas. Spic. Zool. V. 1769, 20. pl. iii., pl. v. figs. 7-9.
Simorhynchus cristatellus Bonaparte. Compt. Rend. XLII. 1856, 774.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific, from Kadiak and Japan northward.

subgenus: Phaleris: Temminck
Phaleris Temminck. Man. Orn. 1820, p. cxii.
type: by elimination, Alca pygmcea Gmel.

species: Simorhynchus pygmaeus: (Gmel.): Whiskered Auklet
Alca pygmaea Gmel. S. N. I. ii. 1788, 555.
Simorhynchus pygmaeus Brandt, Mel. Biol. VII. 1869, 222.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific, from Unalashka through the Aleutian chain to Kamtschatka.

subgenus: Ciceronia: Reichenbach
Ciceronia Reichenbach. Syst. Av. 1852, p. iii.
type: Phaleris microceros Brandt = Uria pusilla Pallas.

species: Simorhynchus pusillus: (Pallas): Least Auklet
Uria pusilla Pallas. Zoog. Rosso-As. II. 1826, 373, pl. 70.
Simorhynchus pusillus Coues, Pr. Ac. Nat. Sci. Phila. 1862, 324.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific, from Sitka and Japan northward to Bering's Strait.

page: 80
genus: Synthliboramphus: Brandt
Synthliliboramphus Brandt, Bull. Ac. St. Petersb. II. 1837, 347.
type: Alca antiqua Gmel.

species: Synthliboramphus antiquus: (Gmel.): Ancient Murrelet
Alca antiqua Gmel. S. N. I. ii. 1788, 554.
Synthliboramphus antiquus Brandt, Bull. Ac. St. Petersb. II. 1837, 347.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific, from Sitka and Japan northward. Accidental in Wisconsin.

species: Synthliboramphus wumizusume: (Temminck): Temminck's Murrelet
Uria wumizusume Temminck. Pl. Col. 1838, 579.
Synthliborhamphus wumizusume Reichenbach. Vollst. Naturg. Vog. Natatores, 1845, pl. iv. fig. 31.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific, from Washington Territory northward, and Japan.

genus: Brachyramphus: Brandt
Brachyramphus Brandt, Bull. Ac. St. Petersb. II. 1837, 346.
type: Colymbus marmoratus Gmel.

species: Brachyramphus marmoratus: (Gmel.): Marbled Murrelet
Colymbus marmoratus Gmel. S. N. I. ii. 1788, 583.
Brachyramphus marmoratus Brandt, Bull. Ac. St. Pdtersb. II. 1837, 346.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific; on the American coast from San Diego northward, and breeding as far south as Vancouver Island.

page: 81
species: Brachyramphus kittlitzii: Brandt: Kittlitz's Murrelet
Brachyramphus kiitlitzii Brandt, Bull. Ac. St. Petersb. II. 1837, 346.
habitat: Kamtschatka and Aleutian Islands, east to Unalaska.

species: Brachyramphus hypoleucus: Xantus: Xantus's Murrelet
Brachyrhamphus hypoleucus Xantus, Pr. Ac. Nat. Sci. Phila., Nov. 1859, 299.
habitat: Coast of Southern California, from San Diego to Cape St. Lucas.

species: Brachyramphus craveri: (Salvad.): Craveri's Murrelet.
Uria craveri Salvad. Atti Soc. It. Sc. Nat. VIII. 1866, Estr. p. 17.
Brachyrhamphus craverii Coues, Pr. Ac. Nat. Sci. Phila. 1868, 66.
habitat: Island of Natividad, Gulf of California.

genus: Cepphus: Pallas
Cepphus Pallas. Spic. Zool. V. 1769, 33.
type: C. lacteolus Pallas = C. gryllei, albino

species: Cepphus grylle: (Linnaeus): Black Guillemot
Alca grylle Linn. S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 130.
Cepphus grylle Brehm, Handb. Vog. Deutschl. 1831, 987.
habitat: Coasts of Northern Europe, south to Denmark and the British Islands. Coast of Maine, south in winter to Philadelphia; Newfoundland(?).

page: 82
species: Cepphus mandtii: (Light.): Mandt's Guillemot
Uria mandtii Light, in Mandt's Obs. Itin. Dissert. 1822, 30.
Cepphus mandtii Bonaparte. Cat. Parzud. 1856, 12.
habitat: Arctic regions of both continents ; south on the Atlantic coast of North America in winter to New Jersey, breeding to Hudson's Bay and Labrador; Alaskan coast, south, in winter, to Norton Sound.

species: Cepphus columba: Pallas: Pigeon Guillemot
Cepphus columba Pallas, Zoog. Rosso-As. II. 1826, 348.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific, southward from Bering's Strait to Northern Japan and Southern California.

subfamily: Alcinae: Auks and Murres

genus: Uria: Brisson
Uria Briss. Orn. VI. 1760, 70.
type: by elimination, Colymbus troile Linn.

species: Uria troile: (Linnaeus): Murre.
Colymbus troile Linnaeus, Faun. Suec. ed. 1761, 52; S. N. ed. 12, I. 1766, 220.
Uria troile Lath. Ind. Orn. II. 1790, 796.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Atlantic, southward on the coast of North America, in winter, to Southern New England; breeding from Nova Scotia northward.

subspecies: Uria troile californica: (Bryant): California Murre
Catarractes californicus Bryant, Pr. Bost. Soc. 1861, 11. figs. 3, 5.
Uria troile californica Ridgw. Water B. N. Am. II. 1884, 483.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific, breeding from California north to the Prybilof Islands.

page: 83
species: Uria lomvia: (Linnaeus): Bruinnich's Murre
Alca lomvia Linnaeus, S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 130.
Uria lomvia Bryant, Proc. Bost. Soc. N. H. VIII. May, 1861, 75.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Atlantic and Eastern Arctic Oceans; south on the Atlantic coast of North America to New Jersey, breeding from the Gulf of St. Lawrence northward.

subspecies: Uria lomvia arra: (Pallas): Pallas's Murre
Cepphus arra Pallas, Zoog. Rosso-As. II. 1826, 347.
Uria lomvia arra Ridgw. Water B. N. Am. II. Sept. 1884, 485.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Pacific and Western Arctic Oceans.

genus: Alca: Linnaeus
Alca Linn. S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 130.
type: by elimination, Alca torda Linn.

species: Alca torda: Linnaeus: Razor-billed Auk
Alca torda Linneus, S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 130.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Atlantic, south in winter on the North American coast to Southern New England.

genus: Plautus: Brunnich
Plautus Brunnich, Zool. Fund. 1772, 78.
type: Alca impennis Linnaeus.

page: 84
species: Plautus impennis: (Linnaeus): Great Auk.
Alca impennis Linnaeus, S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 130.
Plautus impennis Steenstr. Vid. Med. Nat. For. Kjob. 1855, 114.
habitat: Formerly the coasts and islands of the North Atlantic, from Massachusetts and Ireland northward nearly to the Arctic Circle. Believed to be now extinct.

subfamily: Allinae: Dovekies

genus: Alle: Link
Alle Link, Beschr. Nat. Samml. Univ. Rostock, I. 1806, 17.
type: Alca alle Linnaeus.

species: Alle alle: (Linnaeus): Dovekie
Alca alle Linnaeus, S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 131.
Alle alle Stejneger, Stand. Nat. Hist. IV. 1885, 69.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Atlantic and Eastern Arctic Oceans; in North America south in winter to New Jersey; breeds in high northern latitudes.

order: Longipennes: Long-Winged Swimmers

family: Stercorariidae. Skuas and Jaegers

genus: Megalestris: Bonaparte
Megalestris Bonaparte, Cat. Parzudaki, 1856, n.
type: Catharacta skua Brunn.

page: 85

species: Megalestris skua: (Brunn.): Skua
Catharacta skua Brunn. Orn. Bor. 1764, 33.
Megatestris skua Ridgw. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. III. Sept. 4, 1880, 208.
habitat: Coasts and islands of the North Atlantic, chiefly northward. South to Spain and Massachusetts. Apparently rare on the coast of North America.

genus: Stercorarius: Brisson
Stercorarius Brisson, Orn. V. 1760, 149.
type: Larus parasiticus Linnaeus.

species: Stercorarius pomarinus: (Temminck): Pomarine Jaeger
Larus pomarinus Temminck, Man. d'Orn. 1815, 514.
Stercorarius pomarinus Vieill. Nouv. Dict. XXXII. 1819, 158.
habitat: Seas and inland waters of northern portions of the Northern Hemisphere, south in winter to Africa and Australia, and probably South America. Not known to occur in winter on the Atlantic coast of North America north of Long Island.

species: Stercorarius parasiticus: (Linnaeus): Parasitic Jaeger
Lartts parasiticus Linnaeus, S. N. ed. to, I. 1758, 136.
Stercorarius parasiticus Schaff. Mus. Orn. 1789, 62, pi. 37.
habitat: Northern part of Northern Hemisphere, southward in winter to South Africa and South America. Breeds in high northern dis tricts, and winters from the Middle States and California southward to Brazil and Chili.

species: Stercorarius longicaudus: Vieill.: Long-tailed Jaeger
Stercorarius longicaiidus Vieill. Nouv. Dict. XXXII. 1819, 157.
habitat: Northern part of Northern Hemisphere, breeding in high northern districts; south in winter to the Gulf of Mexico.

page: 86

family: Laridae: Gulls and Terns

subfamily: Larinae: Gulls

genus: Gavia: Boie
Gavia Boie, Isis, 1822, 563.
type: Larus eburneus Phipps = Larus albus Gunn.

species: Gavia alba: (Gunn.): Ivory Gull
Larus albus Gunn. in Leem's Beskr. Finm. Lapp. 1767, 285.
Gavia alba Stejn. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. V. 1882, 39.
habitat: Arctic Seas, south in winter on the Atlantic coast of North America to Labrador and Newfoundland, casually to New Brunswick, and on the Pacific side to Bering's Sea.

genus: Rissa: Stephens
Rissa "Leach," Steph. Gen. Zool. XIII. 1825, 180.
type: Larus tridactylus Linn.

species: Rissa tridactyla: (Linnaeus): Kittiwake
Larus tridactylus Linnaeus. S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 136.
Rissa tridactyla Bonaparte, Comp. List, 1838, 62.
habitat: Arctic regions, south in Eastern North America in winter to the Great Lakes and the Middle States.

subspecies: Rissa tridactyla pollicaris: Ridgw.: Pacific Kittiwake.
Rissa tridactyla pollicaris "Stejn. MS." Ridgw. Water B. N. Am. II. 1884, 202.
habitat: Coasts of North Pacific and Bering's Sea.

page: 87

species: Rissa brevirostris: (Bruch): Red-legged Klttiwake.
Larus brevirostris Bruch, J. f. O. 1853, 103.
Rissa brevirostris Lawr. in Baird's B. N. Am. 1858, 855.
habitat: Coast and islands of Bering's Sea.

genus: Larus: Linnaeus
Larus Linnaeus. S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 136.
type, by elimination, L. canus Linnaeus.

species: Larus glaucus: Brunn.: Glaucous Gull
Larus glaucus Brunn. Orn. Bor. 1764, 44.
habitat: Arctic regions, south in winter in North America to the Great Lakes and Long Island. North Pacific.

species: Larus leucopterus: Faber: Iceland Gull.
Larus leucopterus Faber, Prodr. Isl. Orn. 1822, 91.
habitat: Arctic regions, south in winter in North America to Massachusetts, occasionally much further south.

species: Larus glaucescens: Naumann: Glaucous-winged Gull
Larus glaucescens Naumann, Naturg. Vog. Deutschl. X. 1840, 351.
habitat: Pacific coast of North America, from Alaska south to California; on the Asiatic side south to Japan.

page: 88

species: Larus kumlieni: Brewster: Kumlien's Gull
Larus kumlieni Brewster, Bull. Nutt. Orn. Club, VIII. 1883, 216.
habitat: North Atlantic coast of North America, breeding in Cumberland Gulf; south in winter to the coast of the Middle States.

species: Larus nelsoni: Hensh.: Nelson's Gull
Larus nelsoni Hensh. Auk, I. July, 1884, 250.
habitat: Coast of Norton Sound, Alaska.

species: Larus marinus: Linnaeus.: Great Black-backed Gull.
Larus marinus Linnaeus. S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 136.
habitat: Coasts of the North Atlantic; south in winter to Long Island and Italy.

species: Larus schistisagus: Stejn.: Slaty-backed Gull
Larus schistisagus Stejn. Auk, I. July, 1884, 231.
habitat: North Pacific, chiefly on the Asiatic side; Herald Island, Arctic Ocean, and Alaska.

species: Larus occidentalis: Audubon: Western Gull
Larus occidentalis Audubon, Orn. Biog. V. 1839, 320.
habitat: Pacific coast of North America, breeding from Southern California northward.
page: 89

species: Larus affinis: Reinh.: Siberian Gull
Larus affinis Reinh. Vid. Med. 1853, 78.
habitat: Greenland; Asia and Europe, southward in winter to North Africa.

species: Larus argentatus: Brunn.: Herring Gull
Larus argentatus Brunn. Orn. Bor. 1764, 44.
habitat: Old World, south to the Azores; Cumberland Sound; occasional on the eastern coast of the United States.

subspecies: Larus argentatus smithsonianus: Coues: American Herring Gull
Larus smithsonianus Coues, Pr. Ac. Nat. Sci. Phila. 1862, 296.
Larus argentatus var. smithsonianus Coues, Check List, 1873, no. 547 a.
habitat: North America generally, breeding on the Atlantic coast from Maine northward; in winter south to Cuba and Lower California.

species: Larus cachinnans: Pallas: Pallas's Gull
Larus cachinnans Pallas, Zoog. Rosso- As. II. 1826, 318.
habitat: Asia, from the Red Sea to the Pacific and Arctic Oceans; coast of Alaska, south in winter to California.

species: Larus californicus: Lawr.: California Gull
Larus californicus Lawr. Ann. Lyc. N. Y. VI. 1854, 79.
habitat: Western Province of North America, from Alaska to Mexico.

page: 90

species: Larus delawarensis: Ord: Ring-billed Gull
Larus delawarensis Ord, Guthrie's Geog. 2d Am. ed. 1815, 319.
habitat: North America at large; south in winter to Cuba and Mexico.

species: Larus brachyrhynchus: Rich.: Short-billed Gull.
Larus brachyrhynchus Rich. F. B. A. II. 1831, 421.
habitat: Arctic America and Pacific coast, south in winter to Southern California.

species: Larus canus: Linnaeus: Mew Gull
Larus canus Linnaeus, S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 136.
habitat: Europe and Asia; accidental in Labrador.

species: Larus heermanni: Cass.: Heermann's Gull
Larus heermanni Cass. Pr. Ac. Nat. Sci. Phila. VI. 1852, 187.
habitat: Pacific coast of North America, from British Columbia to Panama.

species: Larus atricilla: Linnaeus: Laughiug Gull
Larus atricilla Linnaeus, S. N. ed. 10, I. 1758, 136.
habitat: Eastern tropical and warm temperate America, chiefly along the sea-coast, from Maine to Brazil; Pacific coast of Middle America.

page: 91

species: Larus franklinii: Sw. & Rich.: Franklin's Gull
Larus franklinii Sw. & Rich. F. B. A. II. 1831, 424, pi. 71.
habitat: Interior of North America, breeding chiefly north of the United States; south in winter to Central and South America.

species: Larus philadelphia: (Ord): Bonaparte's Gull
Sterna philadelphia Ord, Guthrie's Geog. 2d Am. ed. II. 1815, 319.
Larus philadelphia Gray, List Brit. B. 1863, 235.
habitat: Whole of North America, breeding mostly north of the United States; south in winter to Mexico and Central America.

genus: Rhodostethia: Macgillivray
Rhodostethia Macgil. Man. Brit. Orn. II. 1842, 253.
type: Larus roseus Macgil.

species: Rhodostethia rosea: (Macgil.): Ross's Gull
Larus roseus Macgil. Mem. Wern. Soc. V. 1824, 249.
Rhodostethia rosea Bonaparte, Rev. Crit. Orn. Eur. Degland, 1850, 201.
habitat: Arctic regions; Point Barrow, Alaska; Melville Peninsula; England, Faroes, Heligoland, etc.

genus: Xema: Leach
Xema "Leach," Ross's Voy. App. 1819, p. Ivii.
type: Larus sabinii Sab.

species: Xema sabinii: (Sab.): Sabine's Gull
Larus sabinii J. Sab. Trans. Linn. Soc. XII. 1818, 520, pi. 29.
Xema sabini Edw. & Beverl. App. Ross's Voy. Baff. Bay, 4to ed.  1819, Ivii.
habitat: Arctic regions; in North America south in winter to New York, the Great Lakes, and Great Salt Lake; casual south to Peru.

page: 92

subfamily: Sterninae: Terns

genus: Gelochelidon Brehm.
Gelochelidon Brehm, Naturg. Vog. Deutschl. 1831, 774.
type: G. meridionalis Brehm = Sterna nilotica Hasselq.

species: Gelochelidon nilotica: (Hasselq.): Gull-billed Tern
Sterna nilotica Hasselq. Reise nach Pal. Deutsche Ausg. 1762, 325.
Gelochelidon nilotica Stejn. Auk, I. Oct. 1884, 366.
habitat: Nearly cosmopolitan; in North America chiefly along the Atlantic and Gulf coasts of the United States.

genus: Sterna Linnaeus.

subgenus: Thalasseus: Boie.
Thalasseus Boie, Isis, 1822, 563.
type: Sterna caspia Pallas = Sterna tschegrava Lepech.

species: Sterna tschegrava: Lepech.: Caspian Tern
Sterna tschegrava Lepech. Nov. Comm. Petrop. XIV. 1770, 500, pi. 13, fig. 2.
habitat: Nearly cosmopolitan; in North America breeding southward to Virginia, Lake Michigan, Texas, Nevada, and California.

Brewster's Linnet.



JEgiothus (flavirostris var.) brewsterii Ridgw. Am. Nat. July, 1872,



433

 Acanthis brewsterii Ridgw. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. VIII. 1885, 354.



[B , C 147, R 180, C 211.]



The type-specimen, taken at Waltham, Mass., remains unique. It

 cannot be identified with any known species, but may be a hybrid be-

 tween Acanthis linaria and Spinus pinus. (Cf. Brewst. Bull. Nutt.

 Orn. Club, VI. 1881, p. 225.)



18. Spiza townsendii (Aud.).



Townsend's Bunting.



Emberiza townsendii Aud. Orn. Biog. II. 1834, 183.



Spiza townsendi Ridgw. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. III. Aug. 24, 1880, 182.



[B 379 , C 192, R 255, C 288.]



The original specimen, taken May n, 1833, in Chester County, Pa.,

 by Mr. J. K. Townsend, remains unique. Its peculiarities cannot be

 accounted for by hybridism, nor probably by individual variation.

21. Helminthophila leucobronchialis (Brewst.).
Brewster's Warbler.



Helminthophaga leucobronchialis Brewst. Bull. Nutt. Orn. Club, I.



Jan. 1876, i, plate.

 Helminthophila leucobronchialis Ridgw. Bull. Nutt. Orn. Club, VII.



Jan. 1882, 53.




[B , C , R82, C TOO.]



Known from numerous specimens, taken in Southern New England, Lower Hudson Valley, New Jersey, Virginia, Michigan, etc. Supposed to be a hybrid between H. pinus and H. chrysoptera, but possibly a distinct species. (Cf. Brewst. Bull. Nutt. Orn. Club, VI. 1881, pp 218-225 ; Ridgw. Auk, II. Oct. 1885, pp. 359-363.)

 Helminthophila cincinnatiensis Ridgw. Pr. U. S. Nat. Mus. VIII.



1885, 354.



[B , C , R , C ioi.]



One specimen taken near Cincinnati, Ohio. Probably a hybrid

24. Dendroica montana (Wils.).



Blue Mountain Warbler.



Sylvia montana Wils. Am. Orn. V. 1812, 113, pi. 44, fig. 2.

 Dendroica montana Baird, B. N. Am. 1858, 278.



[B 199, C , R 112, C .]

Known only from the works of Wilson and Audubon. Taken in the Blue Mountains of Virginia. Not as yet satisfactorily identified with any other species.

 (Nee Gmel., 1788.)



[B 212, C , R 126, C .]

Known only from the works of Wilson and Audubon. Claimed to have been taken in New Jersey and Kentucky.

 26. Regulus cuvieri Aud.

Cuvier's Kinglet.

Regulus cu-vieri Aud. Orn. Biog. I. 1832, 288, pi. 55.

Known only from Audubon's description and figure of the original specimen, killed in June, 1812, on the banks of the Schuylkill River, in Pennsylvania.
